## Document 058: 2026年9月经济数据点评：外强内弱延续，政策脉冲待传导

www.lhratings.com  研究报告 1

外强内弱延续，政策脉冲待传导 —
2026 年 9 月经济数据点评 
联合资信 主权部  
前三季度 GDP 同比增长 4.6%， 三季度回升至4.5%， 经济在二季度减速后温和企稳。9 月
延续 “外需与高技术制造引领、 内需与传统部门偏弱” 的分化格局： 出口再创新高、AI 链主导
且动能向多品类扩散； 消费边际改善但汽车拖累显著， 投资与房地产仍在寻底； 工业增加值加
快，但增长高度集中于高技术制造；PPI 创年内新高，CPI 与 PPI 剪刀差重新扩大；新增社融
超预期但主要由政府债支撑， 信贷仍偏弱。 政策端增量工具加快落地， 但向实体需求的传导仍
待观察。整体处于政策驱动向内生修复过渡的“缺口期” ，修复持续性取决于消费与房地产的
企稳节奏。

### Page 2

www.lhratings.com  研究报告 2 
2026 年前三季度 GDP 同比增长 4.6%，分季度看，一季度增长 5.0%，二季度放
缓至 4.3%， 三季度回升至4.5%， 环比增长1.2%， 经济在经历二季度减速后于三季度
温和企稳。9 月数据延续“外需与高技术制造引领、内需与传统部门偏弱”的分化格
局： 出口再创新高、 工业增加值加快增长， 但消费仅边际改善， 固定资产投资与房地
产仍在寻底，PPI 与 CPI 剪刀差重新扩大，社融总量承压而结构优化。整体而言，经
济处于由政策驱动向内生修复过渡的“缺口期”，修复的持续性取决于消费与房地产
的企稳节奏，而非外需或政策脉冲的单一强度。 
一、出口：再创新高、AI 链主导、动能向多品类扩散 
9 月，按美元计价，中国出口总值同比增长 36.3%，前值为 25.0%；进口总值同
比增长 31.0%，前值为 28.2%；贸易顺差 1335.3 亿美元，较去年同期增加 448.1 亿美
元，创 2025 年 1 月以来最高水平。 
出口的强劲表现仍高度集中于 AI 相关产业链， 但增长动能开始向更多品类扩散。
9 月集成电路出口同比增长 136.8%， 增速较上月加快6.2 个百分点； 自动数据处理设
备及其零部件同比增长 93.3%，增速加快 16.3 个百分点，两者合计拉动出口增长约
13 个百分点。与此同时， 增长动能向更多品类扩散：传统消费品出口明显回暖， 玩具
增长 40.8%， 服装、 鞋靴、 家具分别增长24.1%、20.3%和 22.1%， 服装、 鞋靴、 箱包、
玩具和家具合计拉动出口增长 1.9 个百分点， 低基数效应及对美出口修复可能是重要
原因。 
分区域看，对东盟出口增速升至 52.3%， 对韩国出口增速达68.2%， 均创2021 年
2 月以来新高； 对美出口在低基数效应及中美经贸关系边际缓和支撑下回升至 39.6%，
增速创 2021 年 3 月以来新高；对欧盟出口增速大幅回升至 25.3%，为今年 2 月以来
最高水平， 但9 月中国对欧盟贸易顺差扩大至 348 亿美元， 创有记录以来新高， 需关
注欧盟在 10 月中旬中欧贸易磋商关键窗口期的政策表态是否升级贸易限制措施。

数据来源：海关总署、Wind，联合资信整理

二、内需：消费边际改善但结构分化延续，投资与房地产仍在寻底 
9 月，社会消费品零售总额同比增长 1.1%，较 8 月的 0.4%回升 0.7 个百分点。
剔除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长 3.9%，较 8 月的 2.5%明显改善，反映非汽车
类消费的修复动能在增强。 但汽车类仍是最大拖累：9 月汽车类零售额同比下降 21.2%，
降幅较 8 月的 18.5%进一步扩大。消费数据的边际改善部分源于低基数和以旧换新政
策的脉冲效应， 汽车等大宗消费的收缩尚未出现拐点， 商品消费的内生修复基础仍不
牢固。 
固定资产投资方面，1—9 月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 7.2%，与
1—8 月降幅持平， 跌势未进一步加深， 但仍处于深度收缩区间。 分产业看， 第三产业
投资下降 10.0%， 仍是最大拖累； 第二产业投资下降2.7%， 其中制造业投资下降2.1%，
基础设施投资下降 4.2%。从资金端看，截至 9 月末，新增专项债累计发行完成全年
4.4 万亿元额度的约 76.9%，较上年同期进度稍缓但仍维持较大规模。专项债发行与
投资增速回稳 之间的时滞，表明资金从财政体系到项目落地的传导效率仍是投资端
修复的关键瓶颈。 
房地产市场方面，1—9 月全国房地产开发投资同比下降 20.6%，降幅较 1—8 月
扩大 0.7 个百分点； 其中住宅投资下降20.5%。1—9 月新建商品房销售面积同比下降
13.2%，销售额同比下降 13.9%，投资端与销售端同步走弱，且销售额降幅大于面积
降幅， 意味着房价仍在下行， 以价换量未能有效扭转市场预期。 二手房交易网签面积
同比增长 10.7%，与新房销售的下滑形成分化， 部分需求正从新房市场向二手房转移，
但这并未改变房地产开发投资深度收缩的整体格局。房地产市场的筑底过程仍在延续，
投资端对固定资产投资的拖累短期内难以消退。

数据来源：国家统计局、Wind，联合资信整理

三、工业：增长加快但结构高度集中 
9 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.5%，较 8 月的 5.2%加快 0.3 个百分点，环
比增长 0.61%。生产端的扩张在总量层面是明确的， 但拆开门类与行业结构， 增长的
来源高度集中，传统部门的下行压力并未消退。 
三大门类中， 制造业增加值增长6.0%， 显著高于整体增速； 采矿业仅增长1.3%，
电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增长 5.6%。制造业内部的分化更为鲜明：计算
机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增长 16.4%，专用设备制造业增长 12.1%，均显著高
于整体工业增速。与之对照，有色金属冶炼和压延加工业下降 5.1%，煤炭开采和洗
选业下降 3.3%，非金属矿物制品业下降 2.4%，黑色金属冶炼仅微增 1.6%。41 个大
类行业中 31 个保持增长，增长的覆盖面并不窄，但增速的“高度”几乎完全由高技
术制造和设备制造撑起，原材料部门则在负增长区间继续收缩。 
产品产量数据进一步印证了这一结构特征。9 月汽车产量同比下降 5.7%， 但其中
新能源汽车增长 16.9%；集成电路产量增长 18.4%；工业机器人增长 18.9%。而 9 月
原油加工量下降 8.4%， 水泥下降5.3%， 钢材下降3.8%。与能源转型、 设备更新和出
口链相关的产品保持双位数增长， 与传统基建和房地产周期绑定的重化工业品则延续
收缩。 
一个值得警惕的信号来自产销衔接。9 月出口交货值同比增长 17.0%，外需对工
业产出的吸收仍在发挥重要作用。生产端的景气在很大程度上依赖高技术制造业的高
增长和出口链的拉动，而内需对工业品的消化能力并未同步跟上 。

数据来源：国家统计局、Wind，联合资信整理

四、价格：PPI 创年内新高，剪刀差重新扩大
9 月 CPI 同比上涨 0.9%， 涨幅较8 月的 0.8%扩大 0.1 个百分点； 环比上涨0.2%，
涨幅较上月回落 0.2 个百分点。扣除食品和能源价格的核心 CPI 同比上涨 1.0%，涨
幅与上月相同。CPI 同比涨幅的扩大主要由能源价格驱动。受国际油价高位运行影响，
国内汽油价格同比上涨 16.6%，涨幅较上月大幅扩大 7.3 个百分点，影响 CPI 同比上
涨约 0.47 个百分点。服务价格同比上涨 0.8%，涨幅与上月持平；但环比由上月上涨
0.1%转为下降 0.2%，主要受暑期结束影响，飞机票、宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格分
别下降 8.7%、7.0%和 6.0%。 食品价格同比仍为负增长， 同比下降1.7%， 降幅较上月
扩大 0.3 个百分点，其中猪肉和鲜菜价格分别下降 10.0%和 8.1%。AI 相关高技术产
品价格对 CPI 的正向贡献进一步显性化： 数据存储设备、 平板电脑、 计算机和移动电

话机价格同比分别上涨 46.7%、23.6%、21.8%和 14.9%， 涨幅均有扩大， 合计影响CPI
同比上涨约 0.23 个百分点。
PPI 方面，9 月全国工业生产者出厂价格同比上涨 5.0%，涨幅较 8 月的 3.8%大
幅扩大 1.2 个百分点， 创年内新高； 环比上涨1.2%， 涨幅较上月扩大0.8 个百分点，
由 8 月的微涨转为明显上行。PPI 同比涨幅的显著扩大由输入性因素与国内产业因素
共振驱动。 输入性方面， 国际原油价格高位运行带动石油和天然气开采业价格同比上
涨 24.5%，石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业上涨 21.6%，化学原料和化学制品制造业上涨
12.1%。国内因素方面，煤炭开采和洗选业价格同比上涨 35.3%，有色金属矿采选业
和冶炼压延加工业均上涨 21.4%。 新动能相关行业价格延续上行， 计算机通信和其他
电子设备制造业同比上涨 5.8%，电气机械及器材制造业上涨 6.2%。上述 8 个主要行
业合计影响 PPI 同比上涨约 5.42 个百分点。
CPI 与 PPI 的同比剪刀差在 9 月重新扩大。8 月剪刀差为 3.0 个百分点，9 月扩

大至 4.1 个百分点。剪刀差扩大的方向与 8 月“有所收窄”的趋势相反，核心原因在
于 PPI 的上行动力远强于 CPI。 价格信号的传导仍然不畅， 上游涨价高度集中于能源、
煤炭和有色金属等资源品领域， 中下游传统制造业和消费品行业的价格传导依然有限。
能源和资源品价格的脉冲式上行拉大了工业品与消费品之间的价格裂口， 核心CPI 的
平稳表现则说明终端需求的真实修复仍不足以形成全面的通胀传导链条。

五、金融：社融超预期回升，政府债支撑，私人需求待修复 
9 月金融数据呈现“新增社融超预期回升、信贷总量仍偏弱但结构边际改善”的
格局。9 月新增社会融资规模 3.55 万亿元， 环比大幅增加1.89 万亿元， 同比多增192
亿元， 表现好于市场预期； 社融存量同比增速为7.1%，较8 月末的 7.2%小幅回落 0.1
个百分点。 前三季度社融增量累计为27.46 万亿元， 比上年同期少2.63 万亿元， 其中
对实体经济发放的人民币贷款同比少增 3.04 万亿元，社融口径下信贷收缩仍是主要
拖累。 
信贷需求仍偏弱， 但季末冲量带动环比大幅改善。9 月人民币贷款增加 1.08 万亿
元，环比大幅增加 1.02 万亿元，同比少增 2100 亿元。从结构看，企业端改善较为明
显： 企 （事） 业单位贷款增加1.29 万亿元， 同比多增700 亿元。居民端则持续疲弱：
9 月新增住户贷款为零，同比大幅减少 3890 亿元。消费贴息政策扩围落地和重点城
市楼市活跃度提高对居民信贷形成一定拉动， 但商品消费恢复有限、 房地产市场延续
分化格局，居民加杠杆意愿仍受到明显约束。政府债券仍是社融的核心支撑项。9 月
政府债券净融资 1.53 万亿元，同比多增 3445 亿元，在当月社融中的占比达 43%。与
此同时，政府融资增速结束了连续 13 个月的下行，在 9 月转为上行，财政部已安排
使用地方政府债务结存限额 5500 亿元，四季度政府融资规模大概率高于去年同期，
政府融资的拐点基本得以确认。但企业债券表现不及预期。企业债券净融资当月减少
425 亿元，为年内罕见负增。自 2022 年以来，企业债券融资在 9 月表现较弱，但只
有 2024 年负增长，2026 年再次负增长，这一情况后续值得密切跟踪观察。 
M2 与 M1 增速双双回落，资金活化程度走弱。9 月末 M2 同比增长 7.2%，较 8
月回落 0.3 个百分点；M1 同比增长 3.2%，较8 月回落 0.9 个百分点。M2 增速下滑主
要源于贷款增速回落，信贷派生的货币减少；M2 当月新增 2.82 万亿元，同比少增

5733 亿元。M1 增速的回落则更多受高基数扰动。2025 年同期 M1 当月新增 1.91 万
亿元，显著强于季节性，而本月 M1 新增 9822 亿元，基本符合季节性水平，并非企
业资金活化程度的显著恶化。M2-M1 剪刀差扩大至 4.0 个百分点， 较8 月扩大 0.6 个
百分点，企业活期存款的活化程度仍待改善。

数据来源：中国人民银行、Wind，联合资信整理 
图 5  社融存量增速（%）和增量结构（亿元） 
政策层面，9 月的核心变化是增量政策从“部署”转向“落地”。9 月 28 日国务
院常务会议明确推出增量政策， 次日央行即落地四项工具。 往后看， 四季度财政端的
地方债务结存限额动用构成增量政策的下一个着力点。但在私人部门信贷需求仍弱、
专项债向实物工作量转化尚在途中的背景下，政策加力的效果更可能体现为“托底”
而非“反转” ，其向实体需求的传导效率仍是判断全年经济走势的关键变量。

退坡，2025年9 月8 日

海外经济分化加深下中国外贸动能进一步增强 
——2026 年 9 月进出口数据点评

事件： 2026 年9 月，我国进出口总额为7608.4 亿美元，同比
增长 34.1%。其中，出口 4471.8 亿美元，同比增长 36.3%；进
口3136.5 亿美元，同比增长31.0%；贸易顺差1335.3 亿美元，
接近历史最高点，同比增长50.5%。 
一、9 月进出口数据解析 
◼ 9 月出口增速明显加快，外需改善与科技产业链韧性共同推动
出口高增。8 月我国出口同比增长 25.0%，较 7 月加快 1.1 个
百分点，明显高于2021—2025 年8 月约7.1%的平均增速。9 月
我国出口总体高增的因素有二：一是海外制造业保持扩张，为
出口增长提供需求基础。9 月全球制造业 PMI 由 8 月的 52.3%
升至 53.0%，美国、欧元区、日本和韩国制造业 PMI 均处于扩
张区间，表明海外生产活动仍有韧性，对中间品和制成品的需
求形成支撑；二是前期台风、港口延误及航线调整使部分货物
延后至 9 月出运，叠加海外旺季备货和国庆长假前集中报关交
付，进一步放大了当月增幅。总体看，9 月出口高增既反映内
外需求和生产活动改善，也包含交付节奏变化带来的短期影响。 
➢ 亚洲市场仍是主要拉动力量，对欧美出口同步提速，出口市
场结构改善。9 月我国对东盟出口同比增长 52.3%，较 8 月
加快 22.1 个百分点，出口金额达到 817.0 亿美元，占出口
总额的 18.3%，对整体出口增速拉动约 8.5 个百分点，继续
稳居第一大出口市场。对韩国出口增长 68.2%，对印度、俄
罗斯出口分别增长 47.8%和 51.1%，显示亚洲生产网络和区
域投资需求仍然活跃。与此同时，对欧美出口明显改善：

——2026 年8 月进出口数据点评
对美国出口增长39.6%，较8 月加快5.2 个百分点；对欧盟出口增长25.3%，较8 月
加快18.7 个百分点，二者合计拉动出口增长约7.8 个百分点。其他市场中，对拉丁
美洲出口增速由 17.5%升至 24.7%，对非洲出口虽由 30.9%回落至 16.1%，但仍保持
两位数增长。
➢ 算力相关产品仍是出口增长核心，汽车和传统商品贡献同步上升。  9 月机电产品出
口同比增长 43.7%，占出口总额的 66.7%，对整体出口增速拉动 27.7 个百分点，较
8 月提高 7.4 个百分点。其中，集成电路和自动数据处理设备及其零部件分别增长
136.0%和 92.9%，合计拉动出口增长约 13.0 个百分点，全球 AI 基础设施建设及半
导体产业景气仍是出口的主要支撑；汽车及底盘出口增长 62.0%，拉动作用由 8 月
的 1.7 个百分点升至 2.4 个百分点。与此同时，受欧美市场为年底购物季集中补库

拉动， 传统商品出口呈现普遍改善，服装、纺织品、玩具和家用电器分别增长
23.8%、19.0%、40.8%和 31.7%，塑料制品和家具出口也明显提速，上述六类商品合
计拉动出口增长约 3.8 个百分点。总体看，9 月出口呈现“高技术产品继续领跑、
汽车动能增强、传统商品全面回升”的特征，增长基础较前期进一步拓宽。

海外经济分化加深下外贸韧性能维持多久？ 
——2026 年8 月进出口数据点评 
◼ 进口增速继续加快，生产活动回升和出口产业链备货共同支撑进口需求。 9 月进口同
比增长31.0%，较8 月加快2.8 个百分点，进口金额环比增长11.1%，明显强于历史同
期表现。一方面，制造业生产指数明显回升，出口增速同步加快，带动企业增加中间
品、零部件及能源原材料采购；另一方面，算力基础设施和高端制造投资持续推进，
对相关设备和关键零部件形成较强进口需求。总体看，9 月进口高增主要反映生产性
需求和出口产业链备货增强，传统内需的改善仍相对有限。 
➢ 从进口产品结构来看，高技术产品仍是主要支撑，能源和资源品贡献有所改善但
内部分化明显。9 月高新技术产品进口同比增长 63.9%，占进口总额的 41.6%，拉
动整体进口增长 21.2 个百分点。其中，自动数据处理设备及其零部件进口增长

### Page 6

5 
www.ccxi.com.cn 
海外经济分化加深下外贸韧性能维持多久？ 
——2026 年8 月进出口数据点评 
进口则连续三个月保持较快增长，进出口增长的同步性进一步增强。前三季度我国进出
口总额同比增长 23.8%，其中出口增长 21.3%、进口增长 27.5%，累计贸易顺差同比增长
7.8%。与上半年相比，三季度外贸不仅增速进一步提升，增长结构也由 高技术产品和亚
洲市场主导，逐步转向市场与商品结构多元化。 
◼ 展望后续，全球制造业周期上行、新兴产业需求和贸易关系局部缓和仍将支撑我国出
口，四季度出口增速仍将保持高位，全年出口累计增速有望超 20%，但仍需关注外需持
续性、贸易规则变化以及能源运输成本上升带来的压力。 支撑端来看，全球 AI 基础设
施投资扩张周期尚未结束，仍将对我国机电、高技术产品出口形成持续拉动；9 月下旬
中美就约 300 亿美元非敏感商品达成互降关税框架协议，并开启人工智能领域对话，有
助于缓和双边贸易进一步恶化的风险。10 月上旬，中欧也就我国混动及插电混动汽车对
欧出口开展调节磋商，双边贸易摩擦边际缓和。但也要看到，主要经济体的贸易限制正
由加征关税向配额管理、原产地审查、出口管制和产业规则延伸，政策环境并未实质性
转松。海外高利率、能源和运输成本高企，也可能削弱终端需求并压缩出口利润。后续
出口能否保持较快增长，既取决于科技产业链景气的持续性，也取决于传统商品需求改
善和贸易环境缓和能否形成更多增量。

二、全球经济跟踪：能源冲击推动全球主要经济体货币政策再收紧，增长分化与融资压力同
步加大 
9 月中旬以来，全球经济呈现增长保持韧性与通胀压力回升并存的特征。中东局势推动
油价和运输成本高位波动，美欧日央行相继加息，带动全球长端利率和美元走强，新兴市场
资本流出压力初步显现；与此同时，AI 投资和供应链适应能力仍对货物贸易和部分权益市场
形成支撑，但就业、居民购买力以及运输和旅游等传统行业面临的压力有所加大。总体来看，
全球经济短期内陷入同步衰退的可能性不高，但增长动力进一步向科技相关领域集中，高利
率与能源风险仍将制约需求修复。 
➢ 从经济增长、通胀与货币政策来看，本轮加息并非源于全球需求普遍过热，主要
是为了防止供给冲击转化为持续通胀。 9 月 16 日，美联储加息 25 个基点至
3.75%—4.00%，并将2026 年PCE 通胀预测上调至3.7%、年末政策利率预测中值上
调至 4.1%，政策重心重新偏向控制通胀。当前美国科技投资和居民消费仍有一定
韧性，但就业已边际降温，对后续加息节奏和空间形成影响。美国 9 月新增非农

### Page 7

6
www.ccxi.com.cn
海外经济分化加深下外贸韧性能维持多久？
——2026 年8 月进出口数据点评
就业仅2.9 万人，失业率升至4.2%，高利率以及AI 替代对就业和传统行业的抑制
有所显现。欧洲 9 月 HICP 同比升至 3.8%，其中能源价格同比上涨 18.8%，推动欧
洲央行将存 款便利利率上调至 2.50%；日本央行也在日元偏弱和进口成本压力下
将政策利率上调至约 1.25%。整体来看，主要央行短期内均缺乏转向宽松的条件，
但随着就业放缓、购买力承压，持续加息可能加大需求降温压力，后续政策可能
在抑制通胀与维持增长之间更加审慎。
➢ 从主要资产表现上来看，全球利率中枢进一步上移，市场对高利率持续时间的预
期明显延长。9 月中旬以来，美国10 年期国债收益率由约5.00%升至当前的5.21%，
英国 10 年期国债收益率升至约 5.40%，日本 10 年期国债收益率则在 3%附近高位
波动，反映能源通胀、财政供给和央行加息预期仍在推高期限溢价。同期美元指
数由约 99.6 升至 102.2，进一步收紧了非美经济体的外部融资条件。据 IIF 统计，

9 月新兴市场股票和债券资金合计净流出263 亿美元，为6 月以来首次出现月度净
流出。权益市场方面，受到科技产业景气和企业盈利预期支撑，9 月中旬至 10 月
13 日，标普500 指数和日经225 指数分别上涨约2.9%和6.8%，而A 股与港股仍受
内需修复力度、外部金融条件收紧以及盈利改善预期制约，上证综指和恒生指数
分别回落约1.6%和1.3%。
➢ 贸易和供应链方面，全球货物贸易呈现韧性，但增长动力较为集中，能源冲击对
运输、旅游等服务贸易的拖累仍然突出。10 月 8 日，WTO 将 2026 年全球货物贸易
量增速预测由 3 月的 1.9%大幅上调至 3.9%，并将 2027 年预测上调至 4.1%。上调
预测主要源于全球供应链对能源和运输冲击的适应能力好于预期，以及 AI 基础设
施投资带动半导体、服务器等相关产品贸易快速增长。但这种韧性并不代表全球
贸易已经全面回暖，受高燃油成本、航线调整和国际旅行需求受抑制影响， WTO
将2026 年服务贸易增速预测由4.8%下调至3.3%，运输和旅游成为主要拖累。9 月

中旬以来，布伦特原油价格在约 120—140 美元/桶之间宽幅震荡，当前仍处于约
131 美元/桶的高位，同期原油运输指数由约 4500 点升至接近 8000 点，说明能源
供应和运输风险尚未明显缓解。总体来看，AI 投资和供应链韧性支撑了全球货物
贸易，但高油价正在通过运输成本、航空燃油和居民购买力等渠道压制服务贸易，
全球贸易呈现科技产品偏强、能源密集型行业和传统服务业偏弱的结构性分化。

Takeaways on Photonics from

Morgan Stanley Asia Limited+
Andy Meng, CFA

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line

On Day 3 of the 2026 OCP Global Summit, there is a full-day special focus on
Photonics. Industry participants such as NVIDIA, Meta, Microsoft, Oracle, Broadcom,
Celestica, Arista, Ciena, Amphenol, Lumentum, and Huawei presented their views.
We summarize the key findings below:
1) When the moderator asks key industry players whether they will apply CPO or
NPO, almost all companies answered 'Both'.
2) AI density depends on optical connectivity. With the day's focus on Photonics, the

importance of optical connectivity was well highlighted in each session.
3) Five key metrics for new optical technology: 1) performance, 2) power, 3) cost, 4)
speed to deployment and 5) reliability and serviceability. Huawei believes NPO has
advantages on maintainability, open decoupling, availability and industry
implementation.
4) Scale-in is another new approach to enhance AI density.
5) Root cause of CPO failures: 83-85% of failures due to ELS, 11-17% due to fiber/

connector. Dust and dirt are also common failure issues.
6) Optics vs. Copper: Full Optical Pod is targeted towards scale-up sizes >576
accelerators, Optics and Copper to coexist between 72 and 576 accelerators.
Greater China Technology Hardware: Takeaways on NPO from 2026 OCP Global
Summit (14 Oct 2026)
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
A full day discussion of Photonics further demonstrated the importance of
optical connectivity.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

## Document 178: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 610,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 276,000 원 
상승여력: 121.0%

Signal: 강력한 메모리 펀더멘탈. 파운드리도 수주, 가동률 동반 상승 
Key: 환율 가정 하향에도 27 년 이익 전망 유지. 공급 부족 심화 
Step: 글로벌 최상위 이익 창출, 주주환원. 현저한 저평가

## Document 196: [402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439

Source PDF: all report pdf/[402340] SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대SK스퀘어(402340) 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대하이닉스 자사주 소각_31fbe439.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

SK 스퀘어 (402340/KS) 
하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후가 더 기대 
예정보다 빠른 SK 하이닉스 자사주 매입 
SK스퀘어의 자회사인 SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 20일부터 11월 19일까지 자사주 40
조원(2,407만주, 3.3%)을 매입 예정이다. 9월 29일까지 1,715만주, 예정 매입수량 
대비 71.3%를 매입 완료하였다. 매입 초기 매일 65만주 매입하다가 9월 10일부터는 
60만주를 매입하고 있는데, 이러한 추세가 유지된다면 10월 중순경 자사주 매입이 마
무리될 예정이다. 자사주 매입 후 소각 예정으로 소각이 완료되면 SK스퀘어의 SK하아
닉스에 대한 지분율은 20.0%에서 20.7%로 상승하게 된다.

자사주 매입 이후 추가적인 주주환원에 대한 기대 
SK하이닉스는 지난 8월 19일 정책기간(‘25년~’27년) 동안 발생하는 누적 FCF의 
50% 이상을 주주환원 하는 주주환원 정책을 발표한 바 있다. 26년 SK하이닉스의 예
상 FCF는 164조원(컨센서스 기준)으로 50%를 주주환원 할 경우 자사주 매입 금액
(40 조원)을 제외한 주주환원 재원은 42 조원이다. 잔여 주주환원 재원이 모두 배당으
로 지급된다면 SK 하이닉스 자사주 소각 이후 SK 스퀘어의 지분율은 20.7%이므로 
SK스퀘어의 배당수입도 8.7조원에 달한다. 지난 3월 SK스퀘어는 ‘26년~’28년 경
상배당수입의 30% 이상을 주주에게 환원하는 정책을 발표했다. 보수적으로 30%만 
가정하더라도 SK스퀘어의 배당재원은 2.6조원(현 시가총액 대비 1.8%)이다. SK스퀘
어는 연초 주주총회에서 배당가능이익 확보 차원에서 자본준비금 5 조 8,900억원을 
이익잉여금으로 전입한 바 있다. 이는 26년 재무제표가 확정되는 27년 3월 이후 배당
가능이익으로 편입되므로 SK스퀘어의 주주환원 확대가 기대된다.

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 185 만원(유지) 
SK스퀘어의 순자산 가치 중 98% 이상이 SK하이닉스 지분가치이다. SK하이닉스는 
자사주 매입 이후 17.7% 주가가 상승했으나, SK 스퀘어 주가는 11.4% 상승했다. SK
하이닉스 자사주 매입·소각은 SK 스퀘어의 보유 지분가치 상승뿐 아니라 지분율도 상
승하기 때문에 자사주가 소각될 경우 SK스퀘어의 주가가 탄력적으로 움직일 가능성이 
높다. SK스퀘어 현재 NAV 대비 할인율은 43.7%, 12MF PBR 1.4배로 7월 이후 주
가 조정으로 밸류에이션 매력도 높아졌다. 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 185만원을 유지
한다.

2026-09-30

지주회사/통신서비스/ESG. 최관순 / ks1.choi@sks.co.kr / 3773-8812

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.

당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“With little domestic data of significance,
participants’ attention focused on RBA Deputy

Governor Hauser's fireside chat. Offshore,
China GDP was the key release. "

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.4 to 0.6
Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,
as we expected, though the mix highlights that the waning in
momentum is uneven, centred on investors rather than owner-
occupiers. Business credit meanwhile remains a source of
strength, notwithstanding some slowing recently.
For September, we expect credit growth to nudge to 0.5%mth,
with the divergence between housing credit and business
credit persisting.
AUS: Q3/Sep CPI (%qtr/%mth)

Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.6%, Westpac f/c: 1.3%,
Market f/c: 1.3%, Range: 0.6 to 1.4.
Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.4%, Westpac f/c: 0.6%,
Market f/c: 0.6 Range: 0.3 to 0.8
As noted in the review of the August CPI, our September
quarter CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr holding the
annual pace flat at 3.9%yr. At the margin there was a 0.04ppt
increase due to a lift in auto fuel but it still rounds to 1.3%.
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% highlighting that part

of the increase is due to usual seasonality.
In the month, the September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% with
the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Recreation & culture make
the largest contribution but transport is not far behind. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate increases 0.5%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.
AUS: Q3/Sep Trimmed Mean (%qtr/mth)
Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.9%,
Market f/c: 1.0%, Range: 0.9 to 1.0.

Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.3%,
Market f/c: 0.3%, Range: 0.2 to 0.4
Our September quarter Trimmed Mean estimate is unchanged
at 0.9%qtr with the annual pace dipping a touch from 3.6%yr
to 3.5%yr. There was a small downward revision of 0.03ppt to
0.88% compared to our original published estimate but it still
rounds to 0.9%qtr.
In the month of September we are looking for a 0.3% in
the Trimmed Mean holding the annual pace steady at 3.6%

which will be the fourth consecutive month at this pace. The
distribution of the September Trimmed Mean ranges from
–0.3% to 0.7%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation
picture will probably be judged to have worsened slightly
given recent movements in energy prices and the exchange
rate. A vote seems very possible as differences in view will
likely remain among the MPC about how much the medium-
term inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.
Forward guidance will likely leave open the option to raise

the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy Statement
without presenting it as a sure thing.
NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

### Page 1

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh được 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng 
hơn; trong khi triển vọng nhóm 
khách hàng công nghiệp bị ảnh 
hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của 
mảng này

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 
785 tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An vận hành từ Q3/2025 
giúp sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế 
quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống 
của Việt Nam.

UBND TP.HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng 
mạnh từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh 
trong thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự 
án của doanh nghiệp ở mảng này sẽ vận hành hiệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và 
lợi nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với 
BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với 
giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh 
 Biwase là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực cung cấp nước sạch với 
thị phần đạt 100% tại Bình Dương. 
Ngoài ra, công ty còn mở rộng sang 
lĩnh vực đầu tư nhà máy xử lý nước và 
chất thải rắn nhằm hoàn thiện hệ 
sinh thái dịch vụ hạ tầng môi trường 
tại khu vực. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Nắng nóng thúc đẩy tiêu thụ nước sạch khối dân cư, đem về tổng doanh thu cấp nước tại Bình Dương và 
Bình Phước năm 2026/2027 đạt 2,953/3,192 tỷ VND (6.8%/7.4%). 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Nhà máy nước Nhị Thành giai đoạn 3 đi vào hoạt động giúp tổng doanh thu nhà máy đạt lần lượt 213/247 tỷ 
VND (+16%/16% YoY) trong 2026/2027 và duy trì tăng trưởng trung bình 15% đến 2030.

### Page 4

4

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh 
được thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng 
nóng hơn; trong khi triển vọng 
nhóm khách hàng công nghiệp bị 
ảnh hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan 
 Dự báo đồng thuận xác suất xảy ra El Nino ở mức xấp xỉ 100% từ 2H2026 kéo 
dài đến tháng đầu 2027 cho thấy cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt 
nắng nóng sẽ gay gắt và kéo dài hơn so với các năm trước. Đây là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY 
(+2.6/1.1 ppts). Trái lại, sau khi section 301 được công bố và có hiệu lực thay 
thế section 122, tình hình thuế quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho 
các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống của Việt Nam. Cụ thể, Section 301 không tự 
hết hạn và có nền tảng pháp lý chắc chắn (ít nhất là trong nhiệm kỳ của 
Trump); đồng thời áp dụng hạn ngạch thuế quan cho một lượng hàng dệt may 
nhất định của một số nước không có Việt Nam, khiến bối cảnh bất lợi cho các 
ngành sản xuất truyền thống. Tuy nhiên, với việc Bình Dương đang tập trung 
đầu tư hạ tầng khu công nghiệp hiện đại nhằm thu hút dòng vốn FDI vào các 
lĩnh vực công nghệ cao, sản lượng nước sạch sẽ được duy trì khi tiêu thụ khối 
sản xuất truyền thống được bù đắp bởi nhóm công nghệ cao. Theo đó, trong 
năm 2026, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng nhẹ dự báo sản lượng nước sạch tại Bình 
Dương & Bình Phước đạt 224.9 triệu m3 (+6.8% YoY), với mức tăng của nhóm 
sản xuất/sinh hoạt/hành chính lần lượt là 5.8%/7.5%/11% YoY.

Biểu đồ 4. Xác suất tăng dần của EL Nino

Nguồn: Bloomberg, KBSV tổng hợp

Mảng cấp nước: Tiến độ điều chỉnh 
giá bán nước gián đoạn do môi 
trường lạm phát leo cao 
 Do tác động của cuộc chiến Mỹ - Iran gây ra gián đoạn dòng chảy vận tải dầu 
tại eo biển Hormuz, giá nhiên liệu trong nước đã tăng mạnh từ đầu năm (Dầu 
Diesel - +64.3%, E5 RON 92 – +40%) và khiến cho tình hình lạm phát trong nước 
diễn biến tiêu cực: CPI bình quân 8 tháng năm 2026 tăng 4.5% với chỉ số giá 
nhóm nhà ở, điện nước, chất đốt và vật liệu xây dựng tăng cao nhất với 6.7% và 
giao thông tăng 5.4%. Trong bối cảnh đó, chúng tôi cho rằng tiến độ điều 
chỉnh giá bán nước tại các thị trường của BWE có khả năng bị đẩy lùi sang 
2027-2028 hoặc cho đến khi tình hình giá nhiên liệu ổn định trở lại. Trong dài 
hạn, khả năng đàm phán giá nước thành công là khá cao bởi: i) Thông tư 
145/2025/TT-BTC có hiệu lực từ 2026 là căn cứ để UBND quy định tỷ suất lợi 
nhuận định mức các dự án nhà máy nước; ii) BWE và cổ đông lớn là TDM dự 
kiến đầu tư và mở rộng công suất cung cấp – phân phối nước tại các vùng hẻo 
lánh nên có khả năng tăng áp lực đàm phán tăng giá nước tại Bình Dương. 
Trong kịch bản thận trọng, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng giá nước sạch trung bình khu

vực Bình Dương 1%/năm từ 2028 – 2030.

Đơn giá xử thu gom, vận chuyện và 
xử lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho 
phép cải thiện doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận của mảng này 
 UBND TP.HCM (bao gồm TP.HCM cũ, Bình Dương, Bà Rịa Vũng Tàu) quy định 
giá cụ thể đối với dịch vụ vận chuyển, xử lý chất thải rắn sinh hoạt trên địa bàn. 
Theo đó, quy định mới thay thế văn bản có hiệu lực từ 2016 sẽ chuyển hình 
thức tính giá đầy đủ theo 3 cấu phần: Thu gom, vận chuyển và Xử lý; với khung 
giá được xác định cho các phường đô thị và xã/nông thôn. So với quy định 
2016, quy định mới điều chỉnh tăng mạnh khung giá khoán đối với chủ nguồn 
thải nhỏ (Hộ gia đình tăng 80-184% đối với khu vực đô thị, 55-140% đối với khu 
vực nông thôn; Hộ kinh doanh nhỏ lẻ tăng 42-150% đối với khu vực đô thị, 20-
138% đối với khu vực nông thôn); đồng thời yêu cầu chủ thải tại nguồn và chủ 
nguồn thải lớn áp dụng khung giá thành (bao gồm giá thu gom, vận chuyển và 
xử lý) nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn chi phí liên quan tới các khâu xử 
lý rác thải thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng việc ban hành khung giá mới tính toán 
đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh phản ánh định hướng phát triển 
hạ tầng xử lý rác thải hiện đại của TP.HCM, đặt trọng tâm vào các nhà máy đốt 
rác phát điện thân thiện và an toàn với môi trường. Đối với doanh nghiệp hoạt 
động trong lĩnh vực môi trường nhiều kinh nghiệm như BWE, cơ chế thu gom, 
vận chuyển và xử lý minh bạch sẽ giúp thúc đẩy hiệu quả các dự án mà doanh 
nghiệp có kế hoạch triển khai trong tương lai. KBSV dự báo doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Nguồn: UBND TP .HCM, KBSV

Biwase thành công tăng vốn cổ 
phần lên 2,513 tỷ VND 
 Sau đợt chào báo cho cổ đông hiện hữu với giá chào bán 37,000 đồng/cổ 
phiếu, doanh nghiệp dự kiến huy động được khoảng 1,162.5 tỷ VND, nâng vốn 
điều lệ lên mức 2,513 tỷ VND. Khoản vốn bổ sung được dự kiến sử dụng cho ba 
mục đích chính: thanh toán nợ vay đến hạn (khoảng 773.3 tỷ đồng), đầu tư dự 
án nhà máy điện rác công suất 24MW (225 tỷ đồng) và mua cổ phần tại CTCP 
Cấp nước Ninh Thuận (hơn 164 tỷ đồng), qua đó biến doanh nghiệp này trở 
thành công ty liên kết. Dự kiến, lợi nhuận đóng góp từ công ty liên kết trong 
2026/2027 tiếp tục tăng trưởng tích cực, đạt 238.1/245.2 tỷ VND (40%/3% YoY).

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Về diễn biến thị trường, BWE hiện đang giao dịch quanh mức -1 độ lệch chuẩn 
trung bình 5 năm EV/EBITDA, phản ánh những bất ổn trong thương mại toàn 
cầu ảnh hưởng tới tiêu thụ nước phục vụ sản xuất. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi đánh 
giá triển vọng trong tương lai của mảng môi trường (bao gồm nước thải và rác 
thải) cộng với chiến lược đa dạng hóa địa bàn cung cấp nước sẽ bù đắp được 
KQKD tăng trưởng chậm lại.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA 
với BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ 
vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026. Mức EV/EBITDA mục tiêu cho 
việc định giá doanh nghiệp là 8.8 lần, tương ứng với mức trung bình 5 năm của 
doanh nghiệp, để phản ánh sự cải thiện KQKD đến từ mảng môi trường và cơ 
chế giảm giá mua nước từ TDM sẽ bù đắp sự giảm tốc của mảng cấp nước trong 
ngắn hạn. Với mức giá mục tiêu trên, cổ phiếu được định giá tương đương P/B 
1.7x cho 2026, phù hợp với việc ROE được kỳ vọng cải thiện lên mức 14.1%.

Hệ thống khuyến nghị

Hệ thống khuyến nghị đầu tư ngành

## Document 269: Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone_ Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer confidence falls back (2026-10-02).pdf

### Page 1

September 2026: Marked improvement in

September 2026: Marked increase in inflation,
to a 3-year high
Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas
ECONOMIC RESEARCH
Change since m = 0 = February 2026 / February 2022.

## Document 288: GFHK - Oct Edition

Source PDF: all report pdf/GFHK - Oct Edition.pdf

Equity Research 
Sector Report | Technology｜Oct 15, 2026 
Oct Edition: Choppy Markets, Intact AI

Overseas  
Technology

Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk 
Henry Huang 
SFC CE No. BYA749

Market to remain choppy: Tech equities were volatile in the past month, with rotation 
toward hyperscaler and internet names. That said , s emi and memory (Korea) 
weakness reflected lower risk appetite due to rising bond yields, AI monetization/ARR 
visibility and L-T AI debates (i.e., Broadcom CEO warned that capital may now be a 
bigger constraint), even as company-specific catalysts continued to be strong. On the 
other hand, the positive was the acceleration of AI model scaling (i.e., Meta’s Muse 
Wonder, Gemini 4, etc). That said, w e view the pullback primarily as a reset in 
expectations rather than the evidence of AI deterioration. Despite light positioning, we 
expect the markets will remain choppy in the near term but would  accumulate low 
valuation & sustainable names  (i.e., HBM, non-TSMC foundries, CPUs, CPOs, etc) . 
In our preferred list, we added CLS, while removing MTK and Hon Hai.  
U.S. roadshow takeaways (week of Oct 5): Investor tone skewed neutral to slightly 
cautious, driven by conservative top ‑down macro views, U.S. election, and ongoing 
debates around the L-T AI demand. Positioning in AI infrastructure has come off peak 
levels. Even  so, many investors would accumulate on pullbacks, with interest 
concentrated in “Made in U.S.” names (INTC, LITE, COHR), U.S. memory suppliers, 
TSMC, and selective bottleneck themes — including ASE, ABF substrates, 
MRVL/SMTC, and Japanese MLCCs. 
OCP & earnings season in focus: The week is anchored by AI/semis — SEMICON 
West, OCP, and earnings from ASML, TSMC, etc. OCP reinforced the industry’s pivot 
toward rack‑scale infrastructure, with inference and agentic workloads driving demand 
across compute, memory, and networking. Scale‑up optics, NPO/CPO, and 800VDC 
power were key themes, while AMD’s CPU outlook and Helios/Helion underscored a 
secular CPU uptrend. Nvidia ’s use of PTFE for NVLink boards further supports our 
positive stance on PTFE (see PCB Spec in 2 H27, Oct 5).  On earnings, ASML 
delivered largely in -line results, GM and OP above consensus with revenue slightly 
miss, and raised full year guidance again and is exploring ways to increase capacity 
by >30% 2028 to fulfill strong customer demand, also extending the order visibility 
beyond 2028. For TSMC, we anticipate an inline quarter. 
September monthly sales/data: TSMC sales -0.6% MoM, slightly ahead. Rubin to 
accelerate and T3 ramp. PC forecast trimmed. Substrate/CCL upcycle with 
pricing/margin expansion. Aspeed revenue +101% YoY. Details on page 5. 
GFHK Tech Most Read:  1) Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9 ; 2) Michelle Jing 
on PCB Spec in 2H27; 3) Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration; 4) Jeff Pu on NAND Re-
acceleration; 5) Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview.

GFHK Tech Important Stock Calls  
In the past one month, our key reports and stock calls are: 
Jeff Pu on ASIC Updates on TPU v9, reported date on Oct 13: We expect near-term MTK headwinds 
from TPU v9 delays but remain constructive on MTK/MRVL longer term. Humufish’s redesign could 
push tape-out to Jan -27, MP tape -out to 2Q27 and shipments to 1Q28, while EMIB -T yields could 
recover from 45–50%. Rising TPU allocations to Anthropic could constrain Gemini compute, though 
higher internal allocation in 2H26 may support future models. SemiAnalysis expects Anthropic/OpenAI 
to each add 5 –10GW in 2027 vs. a few GW for Google. For TPU v10 (4Q28 /1Q29), MTK/GUC’s 
Icefish remains the only confirmed mainstream SKU, while Broadcom-led Merope faces AMD/Marvell 
competition, with both potentially testing EMIB-T. 
Michelle Jing on PCB Spec in 2H27, reported date on Oct 5: Some market watchers expect Nvidia 
to shift from PTFE to hydrocarbon resin (HC) for the VR300 NVL576 switch tray. HC—one of the glass 
free alternatives within PTFE hybrid architectures—has only recently passed SI testing and has yet to 
enter PCB MP, leaving it roughly three months behind PTFE in qualification. On the contrary, we 
believe that PTFE offers superior performance for high frequency, high speed designs. With VR300 
NVL576 shipments expected in 2H27 and the final configuration likely set by end 26 —and with a 
leading Chinese PCB ma ker already achieving meaningful PTFE hybrid yields —we maintain that 
PTFE hybrid remains the most probable solution for next year’s switch board. 
Alicia Xia on CPO Acceleration , reported date on  Sept 29 : Nvidia’s CPO scale -out switch is 
progressing faster than expected, with FAU yields improving. We forecast CPO switch shipments at 
15k/16k/30k in 4Q26/1Q27/2Q27 and 100k in 2027. For Nvidia/AWS scale -up, CPO and NPO 
continue in parallel, with full NPO adoption expected at NVL576 launch. Feynman is also devel oping 
both variants, with CPO potentially using QM6 + six 6.4T OEs. We shift AWS Trainium 4 launch to 
1Q28 and forecast global scale-up OE demand at 6.5m/42m in 2027E/28E. 
Jeff Pu on NAND Re -acceleration, reported date on Sept 20 : According to TrendForce, NAND 
demand is stronger than expected as U.S. CSPs scale AI infrastructure, with eSSD demand nearly 
2x prior forecasts. We raise NAND contract price growth to +21%/+17%/+15% QoQ in 
3Q26/4Q26/1Q27, while hyperscaler inventories decline. Supply remains disci plined at 15%/21%/20% 
YoY bit growth in 2025 –27. Demand is supported by agentic AI, vector databases and KV -cache 
offloading, where eSSD offers lower cost/bit than DRAM and better power efficiency than HDD. 
Jeff Pu on TSMC 3Q26 Preview , reported date on  Oct 7 : TSMC shares hit record highs on 
expectations ahead of the print and potential Terafab collaboration. We expect largely inline 3Q26 
results, capex and full-year guidance, with softer 4Q26 margin (67.2%). Management should reaffirm 
2026 capex at the ceiling and mid-40s YoY growth, while we forecast 37% growth in 2027 on sustained 
AI/CPU/networking demand and price hikes. Key focuses include margins, long -term capex/U.S. 
expansion, large-reticle packaging and A14. We raise 2026E/27E EPS by 2%/8% on hig her N2/N3 
capacity and operating leverage, and roll to 20x 2027E P/E, lifting TP to NT$2,960.

### Page 5

Sector Report | Technology

September Monthly Data  
TSMC September sales of NT$511.9bn, -0.6% MoM and +54.6% YoY, with total 3Q revenue reaching 
NT$1.49T, +17.6 QoQ, slightly ahead of expectations of +14%. The solid performance was well 
supported by ASIC, A20 Pro, and Rubin. For 3Q26, we expect gross margin to be 67.8%, higher than 
guidance of 65-67% and consensus of 66.4% . For 4Q26, we expect another 12% QoQ growth with 
GM at 67.2%, both ahead of street’s estimates of +10% QoQ and 66% margin. Ahead of the earnings 
call, key focus include gross margin trajectory, long -term capex discipline, and the execution of its 
U.S. fab expansion. 
Rubin to accelerate:  Hon Hai revenue last month was NT$1.16T, +26 % MoM, +38% YoY, mainly 
driven by iPhone build.  Quanta and Wistron delivered -23% and -17% MoM respectively, due to AI 
racks’ shipping timing differences. Of note, Pegatron surprised the market with +143.2% MoM revenue 
surge fueled by initial GB300 rack shipments to neocloud customers including Lambda and Together 
AI. This reaffirms our thesis of robust NVL72 order momentum across Neoclouds. Looking ahead, 
VR200 is ramping now, with volume deployment accelerating into 2027. We maintain our forecast of 
75k and 92k NVL72 (GB+VR) rack shipments for 2026 and 2027, respectively. Concurrently, Wiwynn 
delivered another solid month with revenue expanding 20.0% MoM, underpinne d by resilient GP 
server demand alongside the ramp of Trainium 3. 
ASIC ramping up: On design service vendors fronts, driven  by T3 ramp, Alchip’s Sep revenue 
reached a record NT$9.9 8bn. Backed by secured incremental 3nm wafer allocation and improving 
yields, management raised its 2027 revenue guidance, now targeting growth above the previous 50% 
YoY baseline. That said, we push out T4 shipment timeline into 1Q28. GUC delivered a strong beat 
with monthly revenue reaching a record NT$7.145bn (+22.4% MoM, +97.8 % YoY), driven by robust 
Axion 2 CPU demand. Beyond its engagement with top 4 CSPs, multi -year order visibility into 2027 
and beyond is further bolstered by high-margin (likely TK1) automotive ASIC designs. 
PC forecast trimmed: NB shipments from top 5 ODMs were 9.7m in Sep, with 3Q26 shipments down 
17% QoQ and 26% YoY. Global PC OEM shipments contracted 20.1% YoY in 3Q26, worse than 
expected, post robust 1H26 pull -ins with high pricing points and weak macro. For 2027, we project 
the industry will decline a further 5% YoY. On the competitive front, tier -1 vendors such as Lenovo, 
Dell, and HP are not immune to this downturn, while entry-level laptops (low cost models from China) 
continue to see stronger relative demand.  
Substrate/CCL Upcycle Intact: Unimicron continued to deliver another all-time high sales with +5% 
MoM and 65% YoY. Supported by ABF upward pricing of ~15% in 2H26 (likely more next year) and 
higher mix of AI products, we expect a meaningful GM uptick for this quarter with momentum to sustain 
into 2027 . In the meantime, ZengDing’s revenue rose 28% MoM and 36% YoY mainly driven by  
iPhone, 1.6T mSAP, and Rubin’s HDI. On the CCL front, bolstered by ongoing price hikes and strong 
demand from GP servers, ITEC revenue grew 15.5% MoM and 111% YoY, and NanYa Plastics 
recorded +12.5% MoM and +55% YoY. Meanwhile, EMC and TUC both posted another single digit 
growth MoM on sales.

Downstream tech – Smartphone & Apple  
We maintain Global smartphone forecast of -14% in 2026  
According to IDC, the preliminary 2Q26 smartphone data points to a still ‑soft market with uneven 
regional dynamics, and their commentary continues to emphasize structural rather than cyclical 
headwinds. Smartphone shipments remain under pressure from weak replacement cycles, cautious 
consumer spending, and limited breakthrough features. IDC typically highlights that “growth is 
concentrated in the premium tier,” driven by ecosystem lock ‑in, while the mass market remains 
challenged by price sensitivity. They also tend to note that China and parts of emerging markets show 
only modest stabilization, not a full recovery, and that vendors are prioritizing margin discipline over 
volume.  
Overall, by factoring in higher iPhone 17/17 Pro volume, we fine tune our global smartphone shipment 
forecast to -14% YoY from -15% YoY. 
For 2027, we expect global smartphone shipments to be flattish YoY. That said, 2026 -2027 will be 
one of the most challenging periods for the industry, driven primarily by sharply higher memory prices, 
which have already pushed OEMs to reduce shipments and raise device prices.

iPhone 18 Pro/18 PM demand lukewarm  
iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max wait-time lukewarm: iPhone 18 Pro and iPhone 18 Pro Max pre-orders began 
on Sept 12 in the U.S. and many other countries. Generally speaking, the wait-time appears lukewarm, 
with: 1) 3–4 weeks for 256GB/512GB Pro Max across all colors, as well as Burgundy 1TB in the U.S.；
2) 2–3 weeks for Glacier 256GB/512GB Pro Max in China; 3) 1–2 weeks for Black/Glacier 1TB Pro 
Max in the U.S. ；4) immediate availability for Silver 1TB and mos t 2TB Pro Max models in the U.S, 
as well as most iPhone 18 Pro configurations except for Burgundy 256GB in the U.S. We attribute the 
lukewarm wait-time to limited spec upgrade, higher pricing (especially for large storage models), etc. 
We also expect the Pro Max to be impacted by iPhone Duo and the criticism of heavy weight.  
iPhone Duo feedback generally positive 
The iPhone Duo, priced at $1,999 in the U.S., opened pre‑orders on Oct 16 and officially launched on 
Oct 23. Early reviews were positive, highlig hting its polished folding experience, high ‑quality inner 
display, and improved multitasking in iOS 27, but also point to trade ‑offs including a heavier and 
thicker form factor, a visible crease, and a dual ‑camera. Due to hinge constraints, we are reducing  
the iPhone Duo EMS build plan from 7m to 6m units in 2026. We also lower the iPhone 18 Pro/Pro 
Max build plan to 72m units given the constraints associated with the adjustable‑aperture camera.

Sector Report | Technology

Downstream tech – PC 
Global PC units down to -12% in 2026, followed by another decline in 2027 
On Oct 8, IDC reported 3Q26 global PC shipments fell 20.1% YoY, missing street expectations of a 
10–15% decline. The sharper contraction was primarily driven by front -loaded demand in 1H26, 
elevated ASPs, and broader macro weakness. This aligns with our prior view that ODM build 
momentum moderated starting in end -2Q26, amid the rising concerns over channel inventory. 
Meanwhile, the top 5 ODMs also saw 3Q26 shipments decline 26% YoY. Looking ahead, we model 
4Q26 shipments to continue 20% YoY drop (flattish QoQ), underperforming normal seasonality. 
On the component front, we expect memory de -spec to normalize. End -users still require sufficient 
DRAM capacity (16GB+) to handle on -device AI workloads, and PC DDR 5 supply constraints have 
eased compared to Server’s. On pricing, new models will still have higher pricing points to pass 
through 2H component cost increases across memory, CPU, etc. That said, we anticipate price hikes 
will remain measured vs 1H26 given sluggish end-market demand. 
Overall, reflecting weaker than expected 3Q26 results post pull -ins and a muted 4Q outlook, we trim 
our 2026 PC shipment forecast to -12% YoY. For 2027, we project shipments to decline further by 5% 
YoY, as higher ASP and persistent macro headwinds continue to weigh on demand.

Upstream tech – AI Semi & Supply Chain  
U.S. CSP capex to be +85%/+53%/+20% in 2026/2027/2028 
Given capex expands further into 2027, CSPs free cash flow are undergoing significant pressure and 
turning negative except Microsoft, such that shifting to debt financing for their massive capex. In 2025, 
Top 5 CSPs’ total debt issuance reached $108bn, or 24% of total capex . Per our Addressing 2028 
Capex and AI Platform Value Shift report on Aug 6, we forecast Top 5 CSPs’ debt issuance to reach 
$257bn/$419bn in 2026/2027, or 31%/35% of total capex.  Assuming debt issuance to grow in 
2028/2029 at a mild pace, we believe U.S. CSPs' balance sheet (except Oracle) to remain very healthy, 
due to net leverage (EBITDA basis) of only 0.5 and the net debt-to-equity ratio of only 18% by 2029.  
Despite healthy balance sheet for most CSPs, we expect most to safeguard balance sheets beyond 
2027 amid widening spreads and FCF profiles, with the focus shifting to returns. Together with a swing 
factor of competition from Chinese LLMs, we expect U.S. CSPs to decelerate capex expansion. We 
forecast Top 5 CSPs capex of $1,512bn/1,739bn in 2028/2029, or +20%/15% YoY, down from +53% 
in 2027.

Server CPU TAM to grow at 49% CAGR by 2030 
As the orchestration core of AI infrastructure buildouts, the Server CPU has gained significant traction 
over the past few months. This is driven by the Agentic AI/inference trend, fueled by the breakthrough 
of OpenClaw and Anthropic’s ARR surging to $44bn  in April. For instance, in latest earnings calls, 
AMD projected a >35% CAGR for Server CPU TAM by 2030, and Intel said the shift toward agentic 
AI is driving a structural demand for CPU, where the GPU-to-CPU ratio is tightening from 8:1 in training 
to 4:1 in inference. Based on our estimates, assuming inference accounts for 90% of AI workloads 
and GPU-to-CPU ratio for AI servers to reach 1:1.2 in 2030, we now expect Server CPU TAM to grow 
96%/71% in 2026/2027, reaching $215bn by 2030 from $30bn in 2025, with a 5-yr CAGR of 49%.

CoWoS tracker: fine tuning upwardly for total capacity 
Following our earlier raise in May Edition on May 12 in which we revised up TSMC’s CoWoS capacity 
from 175KPM to 195KPM by end-2027, the Street has moved up to our numbers , and we have fine 
tune our estimate to 200KPM by end-2027 on our Sept Edition on Sept 14.  
For OSAT, we maintain our earlier view for Amkor’s capacity of 15KPM/25KPM/55KPM by end of 
2026/2027/2028, and ASE’s CoWoS capacity of 15KPM/45KPM/55KPM by end 2026/2027/2028.  
For fabless, we keep our CoWoS estimates largely unchanged for AI GPU/ASIC. For our accelerator 
model, we maintain +98% YoY 2027E, and for 2028E, we now expect it to reach the TAM of $1,000bn.  
In our ASIC Update report on Oct 13, we lowered our TPU units in 2028 to 10m, vs. 8.9m in 2027.

Upstream tech – Optical 
Strong demand for pluggable through 2028. 
We expect that the total demand for 800G/1.6T will reach 90mn/80mn in 2027, increased by 
80%/167%+, mainly driven by: 1) Nvidia, Google and AWS and other ASIC’s Accelerators’ demand 
are ramping up; 2) GPU/ASIC scale -out bandwidth continuously  increase drives increasing 
GPU/ASIC-to-optical module ratio; 3) We expect that TPU for 2027 will be 8.9m and Google fully 
transitions to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up layer will adopt optical interconnects. In 
addition, as we expect that CPO/NPO will start since 2H27, represents a pure incremental opportunity 
for optical interconnects supply chain, benefiting FAU, laser, shuffle box, connector and OE players, 
including LITE, GLW, TSEM, Sumitomo and Browave. We also like Marvell and SMTC, for the 
TIA/Driver used in NPO OE.  
Scale-out bandwidth increase driving optical-module deployment 
For NVIDIA, each Rubin GPU is equipped with two CX9 NIC chips, doubling the scale-out bandwidth 
compared with Blackwell. We further estimate that Rubin Ultra will adopt four CX9 chips per GPU, 
leading to another 2 times increase in scale-out bandwidth, continuing NVIDIA’s consistent bandwidth-
doubling trajectory. Accordingly, we expect the ratio of 1.6T optical modules has risen from 1:2.5 to 
1:5 in Rubin. From CoWoS perspective, we expect 5m/2m Blackwell/Rubin and 20m 1.6T in 2026. 
For Google, we believe that Goo gle fully transition to large -scale clusters in 2026 and the scale -up 
layer will adopt optical interconnects. We believe that in the scale-up domain, the ratio of TPU to 1.6T 
optical modules is approximately 1:1.5; In the scale-out domain, the ratio is around 1:2.5, suggesting 
the overall TPU-to-optics ratio is approximately 1:4 (1.6T equivalent).

Nvidia/CSPs’ Scale-out/up CPO/NPO  
Within NVIDIA’s next -gen NVL576 and broader CSPs’ AI infrastructure plans, CPO and NPO are 
developed side -by-side to expand GP U/ASIC interconnect bandwidth: CPO is the ideal long -term 
design for lower power and latency yet faces headwinds from complicated advanced packaging, and 
yield risks, while NPO could deliver most short -reach optical performance gains with far better 
manufacturability and deployment flexibility, making NPO a reliable near -term scaling path and CPO 
the ultimate end -state architecture. NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal 
conditioning and equalization to system designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that 
take over part of traditional DSP functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens of 
dollars worth of combined TIA and driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics. 
Our shipment forecasts at 6.5m/42.4m NPO optical engines in CY27/28.  
Scale-up domain to increase by NVL576 to compensate lower memory: With lower memory spec, we 
expect Nvidia to increase the scale -up domain by optics to enhance performance. According to 
Nvidia’s GTC 2026, th e optical scale -up fabric allows Nvidia to expand Rubin Ultra to an NVL576 
scale-up. Regarding CPO and NPO which are being parallelly developed, we expect NPO to be a 
high reliability scaling path for NPO. We now expect its scale -up to adopt NPO in Rubin U ltra and 
Feynman platform and estimate OE shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028.

### Page 25

Sector Report | Technology

Nvidia’s NPO OE to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028 
For Nvidia, we now expect its scale up to adopt NPO in Rubin Ultra and Feynman platform and 
estimate OE  shipments of Nvidia’s platform to be 6.0m/19.7m in 2027/2028, based on full NPO 
adoption at NVL576 launch given NPO maturity. Coupled with other CSP’s platform, we estimate total 
OE market to be 6.5m/42.4m in 2027/2028.  
TIA/Driver to benefit from NPO 
NPO partially removes module -side DSP and shifts signal conditioning and equalization to system 
designs, greatly lifting the value of TIAs and laser drivers that take over part of traditional DSP 
functions; we estimate each 3.2T optical engine contains tens o f dollars worth of combined TIA and 
driver chips, a clear improvement over standard pluggable optics.  
A key architectural shift under NPO is the partial removal of DSP functionality at the module level, with 
signal conditioning and equalization increasing ly redistributed into system -level design. This 
significantly elevates the importance of TIA (transimpedance amplifier) and laser driver components, 
which effectively replace a portion of traditional DSP value. We estimate that each 3.2T optical engine 
will embed high tens of dollars of combined TIA and driver content, representing a meaningful step -
up versus traditional pluggable optics architectures.  
From a competitive standpoint, we expect Marvell to remain the primary beneficiary of both NPO OE 
given i ts stronger positioning high speed TIA/Driver. Semtech is well positioned as a secondary 
beneficiary through its exposure to high-speed analog signal chain components, particularly TIAs and 
laser drivers, which scale directly with optical engine proliferat ion under NPO. We forecast 
6.5mn/42.4mn NPO OE shipments in CY27/28. Based on our estimated front-end content per OE and 
a 30% market share assumption for Semtech, we estimate its NPO revenue of $315/936mn. 
Stock Implication 
Generally speaking, scale-up CPO/NPO is an incremental opportunity for optical interconnect supply 
chain, as it aims to replace copper and has no impact on scale -out side. Additionally, scale up and 
scale out CPO solution share the similar suppliers. That said, we expect the key benefic iaries to be 
components such as FAU, CW laser, ELSFP, shuffle, PIC, TIA and Driver. We: 1) prefer Lumentum/ 
COHR due to the demand upside for CW laser, and also like local cw laser players; 2) expect Browave 
(3163 TT) to benefit, given the sizable contribu tion of CPO’s shuffle box considering ~55% market 
share, $5-6k ASP and a volume ramp in 2H26; 3) we also like TSEM, given CSP preferring NPO 
solution for their ASICs scale up interconnection. 4) expect GLW to benefit, given the second supplier 
of shuffle b ox and main supplier of MMC connector/fiber. 5) Marvell/Semtech will also benefit from 
CSP/Nvidia’s NPO solution, since TIA and driver will upgrade in the NPO Optical Engine.
